\displaystyle q(i,j)=\nu_{\alpha}\Big(\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\ \bm{m}_{i}+\bm{\sigma}(\bm{m}_{i})\bm{w}\in I_{j}\big\}\Big),\ \ q_{b}(i,j)=\nu_{\alpha}\Big(\big\{\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\ \bm{m}_{i}+\varphi_{b}\big(\bm{\sigma}(\bm{m}_{i})\bm{w}\big)\in I_{j}\big\}\Big),

which implies
q_{b}(i,j)\to q(i,j)
as
b\to\infty
.
To conclude, note that by the discussion at the beginning,
the infinitesimal generator (hence the law) of
\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}
(the limiting Markov jump process in Theorem
3.2
)
converges to that of
\bm{Y}^{*}_{t}
(the Markov jump process specified in Corollary
3.3
).
Together with the fact that
\lim_{b\to\infty}\big(\frac{2C}{b}\big)^{\alpha}=0
,
in (
D.6
)
we obtain

\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{X}^{\eta}_{\lfloor{\bm{\cdot}}/H(\eta^{-1})\rfloor}(\bm{x})\in A\big)\leq\mathbf{P}(\bm{Y}^{*}_{{\bm{\cdot}}}\in A).

From the arbitrariness of the closed set
A
, we conclude the proof by Portmanteau theorem.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of Propositions
D.1
and
D.2

This section is devoted to proving Propositions
D.1
and
D.2
.
Henceforth in Section
E
, we fix some
b\in(0,\infty)
be such that Assumption
6
holds. In particular,

\displaystyle\text{$(I_{i})^{c}$ is bounded away from $\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)-1)|b}(\bm{m}_{i})$}\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.1)

This allows us to fix some
\bar{\epsilon}\in(0,1\wedge b)
small enough such that

\displaystyle(I_{i})^{c}\cap\big(\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)-1)|b}(\bm{m}_{i})\big)^{\bar{\epsilon}}=\emptyset,\text{ and }\bar{B}_{\bar{\epsilon}}(\bm{m}_{i})\subseteq(I_{i})_{\bar{\epsilon}}\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.2)

To lighten notations in the subsequent analyses, we adopt the shorthands

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{k}(\cdot)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(k))|b}(\ \cdot\ ;\bm{m}_{k}).

(E.3)

We start by highlighting a few properties of the limiting Markov jump process
Y^{*|b}
in Theorem
3.2
.
Recall the definitions of
q_{b}(i)
and
q_{b}(i,j)
in (
3.14
),
and
note that (for each
i\in[K]
)

\displaystyle q_{b}(i)\geq\sum_{j\in[K]:\ j\neq i}q_{b}(i,j),\qquad q_{b}(i)\leq\sum_{j\in[K]:\ j\neq i}q_{b}(i,j)+\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\Big(\bigcup_{j\in[K]}\partial I_{j}\Big),

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\

\displaystyle q_{b}(i)=\sum_{j\in[K]:\ j\neq i}q_{b}(i,j)>0\qquad\text{by condition (i) of Assumption~\ref{assumption: regularity condition on b}.}

Moving on, we apply Theorem
B.1
to show that
q_{b}(i)=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i})^{c}\big)<\infty
.
First,
by Assumption
6
(ii),
the set
\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)-1)|b}(\bm{m}_{i})
is bounded away from
(I_{i})^{c}
,
where
\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)
is defined in (
3.2
).
Next, let
\tilde{I}_{j}=I_{j}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})
, i.e., the restriction of
I_{j}
on the open ball centered at the origin with radius
M
, for some
M
large enough.
It is shown in (
B.5
) that the set
\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)-1)|b}(\bm{m}_{i})
is bounded.
Then, for all
M
large enough we know that

\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)-1)|b}(\bm{m}_{i})
is still bounded away from
(\tilde{I}_{i})^{c}
.
Meanwhile, note that

\partial\tilde{I}_{i}\subseteq\partial I_{i}\cup\partial B_{M}(\bm{0}).

Again, by the boundedness property (
B.5
),
as well as the fact that
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}
is supported on
\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i))|b}(\bm{m}_{i})

(see definitions in (
3.10
)),
we have

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial I_{i}\cup\partial B_{M}(\bm{0})\big)=0

and hence

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial\tilde{I}_{i})=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial I_{i})=0

for all
M
large enough (see Assumption
6
(i)).
This allows us to apply the
C^{I}_{b}<\infty
bound in Theorem
B.1

(by setting
I=I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})
,
and get

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\Big(\big(I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\big)^{c}\Big)<\infty,\qquad\forall i\in[K]

(E.4)

for any
M
large enough.
Then, from the trivial bound

(I_{i})^{c}\subseteq\big(I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\big)^{c}

as well as the bound
q_{b}(i)>0
noted above, we obtain (for each
i\in[K]
)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[K]:\ j\neq i}q_{b}(i,j)=q_{b}(i)=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i})^{c}\big)\in(0,\infty).

(E.5)

Furthermore, Lemma
B.5
verifies that

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i))|b}(I_{j};\bm{m}_{i})>0\qquad\iff\qquad I_{j}\cap\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i))|b}(\bm{m}_{i})\neq\emptyset.

(E.6)

As a result, in Definition
3.1

we know that the typical transition graph associated with threshold
b
contains an edge
(\bm{m}_{i}\to\bm{m}_{j})
if and only if
q_{b}(i,j)>0
.

Next, we stress that the law of the Markov jump process
\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}
in Theorem
3.2
can be expressed using the mapping
\Phi
introduced in Definition
C.4
.
Given any
\bm{m}_{i_{0}}\in\{\bm{m}_{1},\bm{m}_{2},\ldots,\bm{m}_{K}\}
,
we set
V_{1}=\bm{m}_{i_{0}}
,
U_{1}=0
, and (for any
t>0
,
l\geq 1
, and
i,j\in[K]
with
i\neq j
)

\begin{split}&\mathbf{P}\Big(U_{l+1}\leq t,\ V_{l+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ \Big|\ V_{l}=\bm{m}_{i},(V_{j})_{j=1}^{l-1},\ (U_{j})_{j=1}^{l}\Big)=\mathbf{P}\Big(U_{l+1}\leq t,\ V_{l+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ \Big|\ V_{l}=\bm{m}_{i}\Big)\\
&=\begin{cases}\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}&\text{ if }\bm{m}_{i}\notin V^{*}_{b},\\
\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}\cdot\Big(1-\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)t\big)\Big)&\text{ if }\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}.\end{cases}\end{split}

(E.7)

In other words, conditioning on
V_{l}=\bm{m}_{i}
,
we have
V_{l+1}=\bm{m}_{j}
with probability
q_{b}(i,j)/q_{b}(i)
;
as for
U_{l+1}
, we set
U_{l+1}\equiv 0
if
\bm{m}_{i}\notin V^{*}_{b}
(i.e., the current value
\bm{m}_{i}
is not a widest minimum), and set
U_{l+1}
as an Exponential RV with rate
q_{b}(i)
otherwise.
We claim that

\displaystyle\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Phi\Big((U_{j})_{j\geq 1},(V_{j})_{j\geq 1}\Big).

(E.8)

In fact, under the conditions in Theorem
3.2
,
it is straightforward to show that

1.

For any
t>0
,
\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j\leq i}U_{j}>t)=1
;

2.

For any
u>0
and
i\geq 1
,
\mathbf{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{i}=u)=0
;

3.

\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Phi\Big((U_{j})_{j\geq 1},(V_{j})_{j\geq 1}\Big)
; that is, it
is a continuous-time Markov chain with state space
V^{*}_{b}
,
generator

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t+h}=m_{j}\ |\ \bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}=m_{i})=h\cdot\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[K]:\ j^{\prime}\neq i}q_{b}(i,j^{\prime})\theta_{b}(\bm{m}_{j}|\bm{m}_{j^{\prime}})+\bm{o}(h)\qquad\text{ as }h\downarrow 0,

and initial distribution
\mathbf{P}(\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{0}=\bm{m}_{j})=\theta_{b}(\bm{m}_{j}|\bm{m}_{i_{0}})
;
see (
3.14
) and (
3.15
) for the definitions of
q_{b}(i,j)
and
\theta_{b}
, respectively.

For the sake of completeness, we collect the proof in Section
F
.
The representation (
E.8
) and the properties stated above will significantly streamline our proof in this section.

The proofs of Propositions
D.1
and
D.2

hinge on the first exit analysis in Theorem
B.1
,
which is stated for a bounded region
I
.
To facilitate the application of Theorem
B.1
onto the (perhaps unbounded) attraction fields over
f
,
we consider

\displaystyle S(\delta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bigcup_{j\in[K]}(\partial I_{j})^{\delta},

(E.9)

\displaystyle I_{i;\delta,M}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}(I_{i})_{\delta}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0}),

(E.10)

for some
\delta,M>0
.
Recall that we use
E^{r}
to denote the
r
-enlargement of the set
E
(with
E^{r}
being closed),
and
E_{r}
for the
r
-shrinkage of
E
(with
E_{r}
being open).
Meanwhile, define

\displaystyle\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\Big\{t\geq 0:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{j\neq i}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{j})\Big\},

(E.11)

\displaystyle\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\Big\{t\geq 0:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\notin I_{i;\delta,M}\Big\}.

(E.12)

In other words,
{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}
is the first exit time from
I_{i;\delta,M}
,
and
{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}
is the first hitting time to the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of a local minimum different that’s not
\bm{m}_{i}
.

To prepare for the proof of Propositions
D.1
and
D.2
,
we state a few properties of the measures
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}
.
First, since
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}
is supported on
\mathcal{G}^{(\mathcal{J}_{b}(i))|b}(\bm{m}_{i})
,
which is a bounded set (see (
B.5
)),
for any
M
large enough we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[K]}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\ \left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert\geq M\}\big)=0,\quad M>\left\lVert\bm{x}_{0}\right\rVert,\quad\text{ and }\max_{i\in[K]}\left\lVert\bm{m}_{i}\right\rVert<M.

(E.13)

Here,
\bm{x}_{0}
is the initial value prescribed in Theorem
3.2
(and hence Propositions
D.1
and
D.2
).
Next, by Assumption
6
(i) and the continuity of measures,

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(S(\delta)\big)}=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\bigg(\bigcup_{j\in[K]}\partial I_{j}\bigg)=0,\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.14)

As an immediate consequence, note that (recall that
E^{-}
denotes the closure of the set
E
)

\displaystyle q_{b}(i,j)=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(I_{j})=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(I_{j}^{-}),\qquad\forall i,j\in[K]\text{ with }i\neq j.

(E.15)

On the other hand, by (
E.4
) and the continuity of measures,
for any
M
large enough and
\delta>0
small enough, we have

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)<\infty.

Together with (
E.5
) and the trivial bound
(I_{i})^{c}\subseteq\big(I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\big)^{c},

we see that for any
M
large enough and
\delta>0
small enough,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)\in(0,\infty),\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.16)

Henceforth in this section, we only consider
M
large enough such that the claims (
E.13
) and (
E.16
) hold.
Then, given
\Delta>0
, it holds for all
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[K]}\frac{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((S(\delta))^{-}\big)}{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)}<\Delta.

(E.17)

Furthermore, observe that

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial I_{i;\delta,M})\leq\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial I_{i})+\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial S(\delta)\big)+\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial B_{M}(\bm{0})\big).

By (
E.14
),
for any
\delta_{1}
small enough we have
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((S(\delta_{1}))^{-}\big)<\infty
.
This further implies that
the claim
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}\big(\partial S(\delta)\big)>0

could hold
for at most countably many
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}]
,
due to the simple facts that the sets
\partial S(\delta)
are mutually disjoint across different
\delta
’s,
and that
\partial S(\delta)\subseteq(S(\delta_{1}))^{-}
when
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}]
.
Then, together with (
E.13
),
we know that
for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough
, we have

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial I_{i;\delta,M}\big)=0,\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.18)

Here, we say that a claim holds for for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough if there exists some
\delta_{1}>0
such that, over
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}]
, the claim fails for at most countably
\delta
(i.e., the claim holds for Lebesgue almost every
\delta\in(0,\delta_{1}]
).
Lastly, by (
E.18
),
we can pick a smaller
\bar{\epsilon}>0
if needed to ensure that (
E.2
) still holds, and

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial I_{i;\bar{\epsilon},M}\big)=0,\qquad\forall i\in[K].

(E.19)

E.1
Proof of Proposition
D.1

As a first application of Theorem
B.1
,
Lemma
E.1
states that
it is unlikely for
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})
to get close to any of the boundary set of attraction fields or exit a wide enough compact set
before visiting a different local minimum.

Lemma E.1
.

Given
\Delta>0
and
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
,
it holds for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\Big(\exists t<\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})\ s.t.\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\in S(\delta)\text{ or }\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert\geq M+1\Big)

\displaystyle<\Delta.

(E.20)

Proof.

By (
E.17
) and (
E.18
),
it holds for all but countably many
\delta
small enough that for each
k\in[K]
,

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{k}\big((S(2\delta))^{-}\big)\big/\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{k}\big((I_{k;2\delta,M})^{c}\big)<\Delta

and

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(\partial I_{i;2\delta,M}\big)=0.

Henceforth in this proof, we only consider such
\delta
.
Observe that (i) due to
I_{i;2\delta,M}\subseteq I_{i;\delta,M}
, we have

\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})\leq\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})\leq\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x});

and
(ii)
by definitions,
it holds for all
t<\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})

that

\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\notin S(2\delta),\ \left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert<M.

Then, by defining events

\displaystyle A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bigg\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in B_{M}(\bm{0});\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\notin S(2\delta)\bigg\},

\displaystyle A_{1}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bigg\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\notin S(\delta)\text{ and }\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert<M+1\ \forall t<\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i,\epsilon}(\bm{x})\bigg\},

we have

\displaystyle\bigg\{\exists t<\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})\ s.t.\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\in S(\delta)\text{ or }\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert\geq M+1\bigg\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\Big(A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\Big)^{c}\cup\bigg(A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\cap\Big(A_{1}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\Big)^{c}\bigg).

Therefore, it suffices to prove (for all
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\big(A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\big)^{c}\bigg)

\displaystyle<\Delta,

(E.21)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\cap\Big(A_{1}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\Big)^{c}\bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

(E.22)

Proof of Claim (
E.21
)
.
It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in S(2\delta)\bigg)

\displaystyle<\Delta,

(E.23)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\notin B_{M}(\bm{0})\bigg)

\displaystyle=0.

(E.24)

By Theorem
B.1

(in particular, note that the condition
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial I)=0
, under
I=I_{i;2\delta,M}
, is ensured by our choice of
\delta
at the beginning of the proof),
we get (for each
i\in[K]
)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in S(2\delta)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\Big(\big(S(2\delta)\big)^{-}\Big)\Big/\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\Big(\big(I_{i;2\delta,M}\big)^{c}\Big)<\Delta,

where the last inequality also follows from our choice of
\delta
at the beginning of the proof.
This
verifies Claim (
E.23
).
Likewise, Claim (
E.24
) can be shown by combining Theorem
B.1
with (
E.13
).
This concludes the proof of Claim (
E.21
).

Proof of Claim (
E.22
)
.
Let

\displaystyle R^{\eta|b}_{j;\epsilon}(\bm{x})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\{t\geq 0:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\in B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{j})\}

(E.25)

be the first hitting time to the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of the local minimum
\bm{m}_{j}
.
By the strong Markov property at
\tau^{\eta}_{i;2\delta,M}(\bm{x})
,

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\cap\Big(A_{1}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\Big)^{c}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big(A_{1}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\Big)^{c}\ \bigg|\ A_{0}(\eta,\delta,\bm{x})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[K]}\underbrace{\sup_{\bm{z}\in(I_{j})_{2\delta}\cap\bar{B}_{M}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{z})\in(I_{j})_{\delta}\cap B_{M+1}(\bm{0})\ \forall t<R^{\eta|b}_{j;\epsilon}(\bm{z})\Big\}^{c}\bigg)}_{p_{j}(\eta)}.

By Lemma
B.3
,
we get

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}p_{j}(\eta)=0

for each
j\in[K]

and conclude the proof of Claim (
E.22
).
∎

Recall the time scaling
\lambda^{*}_{b}
defined in (
3.12
),
the set
V^{*}_{b}
defined in (
3.4
),
and the terms
q_{b}(i,j)
and
q_{b}(i)
defined in (
3.14
).
Lemma
E.2
applies Theorem
B.1

to obtain first exit analyses for each attraction field over
f
.

Lemma E.2
.

Let
\bar{\epsilon}>0
be specified as in (
E.2
).

1.

Let
R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})
be defined as in the (
E.25
).
For any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
,
t>0
and
i\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in((I_{i})_{\epsilon}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0}))^{-}}\mathbf{P}\bigg({R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\leq t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\in I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\ \forall t\leq{R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})}\bigg)=1.

2.

Let
i,j\in[K]
be such that
i\neq j
.
Let
{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}
be defined as in (
E.11
).
If
\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}
, then for any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
and any
u>0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}>u,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot u\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)},

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}>u,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot u\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}.

If
\bm{m}_{i}\notin V^{*}_{b}
, then for any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
and any
u>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}

\displaystyle\leq\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\leq u,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\leq u,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)\leq\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}.

Proof.

(i)

Recall the notations in (
E.3
),
and that

\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1}(\eta)
as
\eta\downarrow 0
(see (
3.12
)).
Due to
\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1\geq\alpha>1
,
given any
T>0

we have

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{T/\eta}{t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}=0,

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg({R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\leq t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\in I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\ \forall u\leq R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbf{P}\bigg({R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})}\leq T/\eta,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\in I_{i}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})\ \forall u\leq R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})\bigg)

for all
\eta
small enough.
Applying Lemma
B.3
onto the bounded region
(I_{i})_{\epsilon}\cap B_{M}(\bm{0})
and sending
T\to\infty
,
we conclude the proof of part (i).

(ii)

Let
\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\eta\cdot\big(\lambda(\eta)\big)^{\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)}
,
where
\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)
is defined in (
3.2
).
To prove part (ii),
it suffices to establish the following upper and lower bounds: given
i,j\in[K]
such that
i\neq j
, and
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
,
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot t\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)},

(E.26)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot t\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}.

(E.27)

To see why, note that in case of
\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}
,
the claims in part (ii) are equivalent to (
E.26
) and (
E.27
)
due to
\mathcal{J}_{b}(i)=\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}

and hence
\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)=\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)

(see (
3.2
) and (
3.3
)).
In case that
\bm{m}_{i}\notin V^{*}_{b}
,
we have

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{t/\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}{u/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}=0

for any
t,u\in(0,\infty)
.
We then recover the upper and lower bounds in part (ii) by sending
t\downarrow 0
in (
E.26
) and (
E.27
).

The rest of this proof is devoted to establishing (
E.26
) and (
E.27
).
We begin by stating few useful facts about the measures
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{k}
.
Combining (
E.15
)
with the continuity of measures,
we get

\lim_{\delta\downarrow 0}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(((I_{i})_{\delta})^{c}\big)=q_{b}(i)=\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(I_{i}^{c}).

Given any
\Delta>0
,
by (
E.17
) it then holds all
\delta>0
small enough,

\begin{split}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)&\leq\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((B_{M}(\bm{0}))^{c}\big)+\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big(((I_{i})_{\delta})^{c}\big)<(1+\Delta)\cdot q_{b}(i),\quad\forall i\in[K].\end{split}

(E.28)

Besides,
due to
I_{i;\delta,M}\subseteq I_{i}
,

\displaystyle\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)\geq\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i})^{c}\big)=q_{b}(i).

(E.29)

Lastly, recall that by (
E.18
),
the condition
\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(\partial I_{i;\delta,M})=0\ \forall i\in[K]
holds for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough,
which supports the application of Theorem
B.1
in the subsequent proof.

Proof of Lower Bound (
E.26
)
.
We fix some
i\neq j
and
t>0
when proving (
E.26
).
By the definition of
\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})

in
(
E.12
),

\displaystyle{\big\{{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\big\}}

\displaystyle\supseteq\underbrace{\big\{{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j;\delta,M+1}\big\}}_{\text{(I)}}\cap\underbrace{\big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\big\}}_{\text{(II)}}.

We first analyze
\mathbf{P}(\text{(II)}|\text{(I)})
.
By the strong Markov property at
\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})
,
we have

\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(II)}\ |\ \text{(I)})\geq\inf_{\bm{y}\in I_{j;\delta,M+1}}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{y})\in I_{j}\ \forall t\leq{R^{\eta|b}_{j;\epsilon}(\bm{y})}\big),

where

{R_{j;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{y})}

is defined in (
E.25
)
and the set
I_{j;\delta,N}
is defined in (
E.10
).
Applying Lemma
B.3
, we yield

\begin{split}&\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(II)}\ |\ \text{(I)})=1.\end{split}

(E.30)

Next,
due to
I_{j;\delta,M+1}\subseteq I_{j}
,

(I)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big\{{\tau^{\eta}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\big\}}_{\text{(III)}}\cap\underbrace{\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j;\delta,M+1}\}}_{\text{(IV)}}.

Given any
\Delta>0
, observe that (for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(III)})

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c})\cdot t\big)\cdot\frac{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}(I_{j})}{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c})}\quad\text{ by Theorem~\ref{theorem: first exit analyses, adapted}}

\displaystyle>\frac{\exp(-(1+\Delta)q_{b}(i)\cdot t)}{1+\Delta}\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}\qquad\text{for any $\delta>0$ small enough, due to
\eqref{proof, bound for measure check C, proposition: transition time, metastability}.}

Meanwhile,
by Lemma
E.1
,
we have

\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(IV)}^{c})<\Delta

for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough.
In summary, given
\Delta>0
, one can find
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\inf_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(I)})\geq\frac{\exp(-(1+\Delta)q_{b}(i)\cdot t)}{1+\Delta}\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}-\Delta.

(E.31)

Combining (
E.30
) and (
E.31
)
and sending
\Delta\downarrow 0
,
we establish the lower bound (
E.26
).

Proof of Upper Bound (
E.27
)
.
Let
\text{(I)}=\{{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\}
.
Given
\delta>0
,
define the event
\text{(II)}=\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in B_{M+1}(\bm{0})\setminus S(\delta)\}
,
where
S(\delta)
is defined in (
E.9
).
Pick some
\Delta>0
, and note the decomposition of events
\text{(I)}=\big(\text{(I)}\setminus\text{(II)}\big)\cup\big(\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\big)
.
Applying Lemma
E.1
,
it holds for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(I)}\setminus\text{(II)})\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}(\text{(II)}^{c})<\Delta.

(E.32)

Next, by the definition of
{\tau^{\eta}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}
in (
E.12
),
on the event
\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}
there must be some
k\in[K]
with
k\neq i
such that

\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}.

For each
k\in[K]
with
k\neq i
, let

\text{($k$)}=\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\cap\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}\}.

Note that
\bigcup_{k\in[K]:\ k\neq i}\text{($k$)}=\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}
.
To proceed, consider the following decomposition

\displaystyle(k)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\bigg((k)\cap\Big\{\Big(\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})-\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})\Big)\cdot\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)>\Delta\Big\}\bigg)}_{(k,1)}\cup\underbrace{\bigg((k)\cap\Big\{\Big(\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})-\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})\Big)\cdot\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)\leq\Delta\Big\}\bigg)}_{(k,2)}.

To proceed, we fix some
k\in[K]
with
k\neq i
.
First,
due to

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{T/\eta}{\Delta/\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}=0

for any
T\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\big((k,1)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\Big((k)\cap\{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})-\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})>T/\eta\}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{y}\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}}\mathbf{P}(\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{y})>T/\eta)\qquad\text{by the definition of the event $(k)$ and strong Markov property}

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{y}\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{y})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k})\ \forall t\leq T/\eta\Big)\qquad\text{due to }k\neq i

\displaystyle=0\qquad\text{for any $T>0$ large enough, due to Lemma \ref{lemma: cycle, efficient return}.}

(E.33)

Meanwhile,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\big((k,2)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\ \mathbf{P}\Big({\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t-\Delta;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k;\delta,M+1};\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\ \mathbf{P}\Big({\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t-\Delta;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\ \mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\ \Big|\ {\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t-\Delta;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\ \mathbf{P}\Big(\underbrace{{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t-\Delta;\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\tau^{\eta|b}_{i;\delta,M}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{k}}_{\text{($k$,I)}}\Big)\cdot\sup_{\bm{y}\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}}\mathbf{P}\Big(\underbrace{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{y})}(\bm{y})\in I_{j}}_{\text{($k$,II)}}\Big).

Applying Theorem
B.1
onto
I_{i;\delta,M}
, we yield (for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough)

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{($k$,I)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\Big(-\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)\cdot(t-\Delta)\Big)\cdot\frac{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{k})^{-}\big)}{\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{i}\big((I_{i;\delta,M})^{c}\big)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot(t-\Delta)\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,k)}{q_{b}(i)}\quad\text{by \eqref{proof, bound for measure check C, 2, proposition: transition time, metastability} and \eqref{proof, mass I j = I j closure, proposition: transition time, metastability}.}

(E.34)

Next, we analyze the probability of event (
k
,II).
If
k=j
, we plug in the trivial upper bound
\mathbf{P}(\text{($k$,II)})\leq 1
.
If
k\neq j
,
on the event (
k
,II), we have that
\big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{y})\big)_{t\geq 0}
visited
B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{j})
before visiting the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of any other local minima, despite the fact that the initial value
\bm{y}
belongs to
I_{k;\delta,M+1}\subset I_{k}
.
Then, by Lemma
B.3
,
for any
\delta>0
small enough (so that
I_{k;\delta,M}\neq\emptyset
) we have

\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{y}\in I_{k;\delta,M+1}}\mathbf{P}(\text{($k$,II)})=0\ \forall k\neq j.

Combining (
E.32
)–(
E.34
),
we get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\Big({\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b;i}(\eta)}>t,\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}\Big)\leq\Delta+\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)\cdot(t-\Delta)\big)\cdot\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}.

Sending
\Delta\downarrow 0
, we conclude the proof of the upper bound (
E.27
).
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
D.1
.

Proof of Proposition
D.1
.

Recall the definitions in (
D.1
)–(
D.4
), and let

\displaystyle U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big({\tau^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x}_{0})-\tau^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k-1}(\bm{x}_{0})}\big)\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)},\qquad V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bm{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x}_{0})};

We first show that claims
(i)
and
(ii)
follow directly from the next claim:
for any
\epsilon>0
small enough,

\displaystyle(U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1},V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1},U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{2},V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{2},\cdots)\Rightarrow(U_{1},V_{2},U_{2},V_{2},\cdots)\qquad\text{ as }\eta\downarrow 0,

(E.35)

where the law of
U_{j}
’s and
V_{j}
’s are defined in (
E.7
).
Specifically, we only consider
\epsilon>0
small enough such that claim (
E.35
) holds.
In light of Lemma
C.5
and Proposition
F.1
,
(
E.35
) verifies part
(i)
of Proposition
D.1
.
Regarding part
(ii)
, note that
\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}
in (
D.5
) is a step function (i.e., piece-wise constant) that only takes values in
\mathcal{M}=\{\bm{m}_{j}:\ j=1,2,\cdots,K\}
, which is a finite set.
Let

\displaystyle A_{N}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{\xi\in\mathbb{D}[0,T]:\ \xi\text{ is a step function with at most $N$ jumps and only takes values in }\mathcal{M}\}.

First, the finite-dimensional nature of
A_{N}
(i.e., at most
N
jumps over
[0,T]
, only
K
possible values) implies that
A_{N}
is a compact set in
(\mathbb{D}[0,T],\bm{d}_{L_{p}}^{{}_{[0,T]}})
.
Besides,

\displaystyle\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta_{n},\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x}_{0})\notin A_{N})

\displaystyle=\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}U^{\eta_{n},\epsilon}_{j}\leq T)\leq\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j=1}^{N+1}U_{j}\leq T),

where the last inequality follows from
(U^{\eta_{n},\epsilon}_{1},\cdots,U^{\eta_{n},\epsilon}_{N})\Rightarrow(U_{1},\cdots,U_{N})
.
By part
(i)
of Proposition
F.1
,
we confirm that
\lim_{N\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta_{n},\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x}_{0})\notin A_{N})=0
,
which verifies the tightness of
\big(\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta_{n},\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x}_{0})\big)_{n\geq 1}
with
\eta_{n}\downarrow 0
.

Now, it only remains to prove (
E.35
).
This is equivalent to proving that, for each
N\geq 1
,

(U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1},V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1},\cdots,U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{N},V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{N})
converges in distribution to
(U_{1},V_{1},\cdots,U_{N},V_{N})
as
\eta\downarrow 0
.
Fix some
N=1,2,\cdots
.
First, by definitions we have
U_{1}=0
and
V_{1}=\bm{m}_{i_{0}}
.
From part
(i)
of Lemma
E.2
,
we get
(U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1},V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{1})\Rightarrow(0,\bm{m}_{i})=(U_{1},V_{1})
as
\eta\downarrow 0
.
Next, for any
n\geq 1
, any
t_{l}\in(0,\infty)
, any sequence
v_{l}\in\{\bm{m}_{i}:\ i\in[K]\}
, and any
t>0
,
i,j\in[K]
with
i\neq j
,
it follows from part
(ii)
of Lemma
E.2
that

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\bigg(U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{n+1}\leq t,\ V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{n+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ \bigg|\ V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{n}=\bm{m}_{i},\ V^{\eta,\epsilon}_{l}=v_{l}\ \forall l\in[n-1],\ U^{\eta,\epsilon}_{l}\leq t_{l}\ \forall l\in[n]\bigg)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}&\text{ if }\bm{m}_{i}\notin V^{*}_{b},\\
\frac{q_{b}(i,j)}{q_{b}(i)}\cdot\Big(1-\exp\big(-q_{b}(i)t\big)\Big)&\text{ if }\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}.\end{cases}

This coincides with the conditional law of
\mathbf{P}\Big(U_{n+1}\leq t,\ V_{n+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ \Big|\ V_{n}=\bm{m}_{i},\ (V_{j})_{j=1}^{n-1},\ (U_{j})_{j=1}^{n}\Big)
specified in (
E.7
).
By arguing inductively, we conclude the proof.
∎

E.2
Proof of Proposition
D.2

Moving onto the proof of Proposition
D.2
,
we first prepare a lemma that establishes the weak convergence from
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor{\bm{\cdot}}/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})
to
\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x})

in terms of finite dimensional distributions.

Lemma E.3
.

Given any
t>0
and
\bm{x}\in\bigcup_{i\in[K]}I_{i}
with
\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert<M
,

1.

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\right\rVert>M\text{ for some }s\leq t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\Big)=0;

2.

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert\geq\epsilon\Big)=0

for all
\epsilon>0
small enough.

Proof.

Throughout this proof, let
\bar{\epsilon}
be specified as in (
E.2
).

(i)
We prove a stronger result.
Let
I^{(M,\delta)}=B_{M}(\bm{0})\setminus S(\delta)
,
where
S(\delta)
is the
\delta
-enlargement of the boundary sets defined in (
E.9
).
Recall the definition of
\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x})
in (
D.1
) and (
D.3
).
For each
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, on the event

\displaystyle\Bigg(\bigcap_{k=0}^{N-1}\underbrace{\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\in I^{(M,\delta)}\ \forall s\in\big[\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x}),\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k+1}(\bm{x})\big]\Big\}}_{A_{k}(\eta,\delta)}\Bigg)\cap\underbrace{\Big\{\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x})\leq t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\Big\}}_{B_{1}(\eta)}\cap\underbrace{\Big\{\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{N}(\bm{x})>t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\Big\}}_{B_{2}(\eta)},

we have
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\in I^{(M,\delta)}
for all
s\in[\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x}),\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{N}(\bm{x})]
and
\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x})\leq t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)<\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{N}(\bm{x})
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that given any
\Delta>0
, there exist
N
and
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(B_{1}(\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)+\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(B_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A_{k}(\eta,\delta)\big)^{c}\Big)<\Delta.

(E.36)

Let
i\in[K]
be the unique index such that
\bm{x}\in I_{i}

and let

R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})
be the first hitting time to the
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\bm{m}_{i}
(see (
E.25
)).
Since
\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x})
is the first visit time to
\bigcup_{l\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l})
(see (
D.1
)),
we have
\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x})\leq R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})
and hence

\begin{split}\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(B_{1}(\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)&=\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{1}(\bm{x})>t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\Big)\leq\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\cdot R_{i;\epsilon}^{\eta|b}(\bm{x})>t\Big)\\
&=0\qquad\text{using Lemma~\ref{proposition: transition time, metastability} $(i)$}.\end{split}

(E.37)

Next, for the limiting process
\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}
in Theorem
3.2
,
recall that we have collected a few important properties at the beginning of Section
E
(with detailed proofs deferred to Section
F
).
In particular, for the
U_{j}
’s defined in (
E.7
),
we can fix some
N
large enough such that

\mathbf{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{N}\leq t)<\Delta/2.

Then, by the proof of Proposition
D.1
above,

\begin{split}\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(B_{2}(\eta)\big)^{c}\Big)&=\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\big({\tau^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k+1}(\bm{x})-\tau^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x})}\big)\cdot{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\leq t\Bigg)\\
&\leq\mathbf{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{N}\leq t)<\Delta/2.\end{split}

(E.38)

Meanwhile, recall the definition of

\sigma^{\eta|b}_{i;\epsilon}(\bm{x})=\min\{s\geq 0:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{l\neq i}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l})\}
in (
E.11
).
By the strong Markov property at
\hat{\tau}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{k}(\bm{x})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 1}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A_{k}(\eta,\delta)\big)^{c}\Big)\leq\max_{l\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{y}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\exists t<\sigma^{\eta|b}_{l;\epsilon}(\bm{y})\ s.t.\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{y})\in S(\delta)\text{ or }\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{y})\right\rVert>M\bigg).

By Lemma
E.1
,
for all but countably many
\delta>0
small enough we have

\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\big(A_{k}(\eta,\delta)\big)^{c}\big)\leq\frac{\Delta}{2N}\ \forall k\in[N-1]
.
Likewise, the case of
k=0
can be bounded using part
(i)
of Lemma
E.2
.
Combining this bound with (
E.37
) and (
E.38
),
we finish the proof of (
E.36
).

(ii)

If
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{l\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l})
,
then by the definition of
\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x})
as the marker of the last visited local minimum (see (
D.1
)–(
D.5
)),
we must have
\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x})\right\rVert<\epsilon
.
Therefore, it suffices to show that for any
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})

(where
\bar{\epsilon}
is characterized in (
E.2
))

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{l\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l})\Big)=1.

Pick some
\delta_{t}\in(0,\frac{t}{3})
,
\delta>0
.
Recall that
H(\cdot)=\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{1}\right\rVert>\cdot)
, and define the event

\text{(I)}=\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-\lfloor{2\delta_{t}}/{H(\eta^{-1})}\rfloor}(\bm{x})\in I^{(M,\delta)}\Big\}.

Let
t_{1}(\eta)=\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-\lfloor{2\delta_{t}}/{H(\eta^{-1})}\rfloor
.
On the event (I), let

R^{\eta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\bigg\{s\geq t_{1}(\eta):\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{l\in[K]}B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{l})\bigg\},

and set
\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}
by the rule

\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}=j\iff\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{R^{\eta}}(\bm{x})\in I_{j}.

Then, we define the event

\displaystyle\text{(II)}=\Big\{R^{\eta}-t_{1}(\eta)\leq{\delta_{t}}/{H(\eta^{-1})}\Big\}.

On the event
\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}
, note that

\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-\lfloor{2\delta_{t}}/{H(\eta^{-1})}\rfloor\leq R^{\eta}\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor.

Furthermore,
let
\tau^{\eta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\{s\geq R^{\eta}:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{\hat{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}})\}
,
and define event

\displaystyle\text{(III)}=\Big\{\tau^{\eta}-R^{\eta}>2\delta_{t}/H(\eta^{-1})\Big\}.

On the event
\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\cap\text{(III)}
, we must have
\tau^{\eta}>\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor\geq R^{\eta}
,
and hence

\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\in\bigcup_{l\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{l}).

Therefore, suppose that given each
\Delta>0
there exist
\delta_{t}\in(0,\frac{t}{3})
and
\delta>0
such that

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(I)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Delta,

(E.39)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(II)}\ \big|\ \text{(I)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1,

(E.40)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\text{(III)}\ \big|\ \text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Delta.

(E.41)

Then, we immediately get
\liminf_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}(\text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\cap\text{(III)})\geq(1-\Delta)^{2}
.
Sending
\Delta\downarrow 0
, we conclude the proof.
The rest of this proof is devoted to establishing
(
E.39
)
(
E.40
)
(
E.41
),
where we fix some
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
and
\Delta>0
.

Proof of (
E.39
)
.
This has been established in the proof for part
(i)
.

Proof of (
E.40
)
.
This claim holds for any
\delta_{t}\in(0,t/3)
,
and can be obtained by combining Lemma
B.3

with the preliminary fact that, given each
T>0
, the inequality
T/\eta<\delta_{t}/H(\eta^{-1})
holds for all
\eta
small enough (due to
H(\eta^{-1})\in\mathcal{RV}_{\alpha}(\eta)
as
\eta\downarrow 0
with
\alpha>1
).

Proof of (
E.41
)
.
By the strong Markov property at
R^{\eta}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{(III)}^{c}\ \Big|\ \text{(I)}\cap\text{(II)}\Big)\leq\max_{k\in[K]}\sup_{\bm{y}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{k})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\exists s\leq\frac{2\delta_{t}}{H(\eta^{-1})}\ s.t.\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{s}(\bm{y})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k})\bigg).

(E.42)

Also, note that
\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}<b
; see (
E.2
).
For each
k\in[K]
, by Theorem
B.1
under the choice of
I=B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k})
,
we obtain some
c_{k,\epsilon}\in(0,\infty)

such that for any
u>0
,

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{y}\in\bar{B}_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{k})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\exists j\leq\frac{u}{H(\eta^{-1})}\ s.t.\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{j}(\bm{y})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k})\bigg)\leq 1-\exp(-c_{k,\epsilon}\cdot u).

(E.43)

By picking
\delta_{t}
small enough, we ensure that
\max_{k\in[K]}1-\exp(-c_{k,\epsilon}\cdot 2\delta_{t})<\Delta
,
thus completing the proof of claim (
E.41
).
To conclude, we elaborate a bit more on the constant
c_{\epsilon}
and the application of Theorem
B.1
above.
The event on the RHS of (
E.42
)
is about the exit from an
\epsilon
-neighborhood of
\bm{m}_{k}
.
Due to
\epsilon<\bar{\epsilon}<b
, this can be achieved by one jump (in the sense of
\mathcal{J}^{I}_{b}
in Theorem
B.1
) if we start from
\bm{m}_{j}
.
Specifically, adopting the notations in Theorem
B.1
,
we let

\displaystyle c_{k,\epsilon}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}^{(1)|b}\big((B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k}))^{c}\big)=\int\mathbf{I}\Big\{w\cdot\left\lVert\bm{\sigma}(\bm{m}_{j})\bm{\theta}\right\rVert>\epsilon\Big\}\nu_{\alpha}(dw)\mathbf{S}(d\theta),

where the equality follows from (
3.10
).
On one hand, the non-degeneracy of
\bm{\sigma}(\cdot)
(see Assumption
4
)
implies that
\inf_{\left\lVert\bm{\theta}\right\rVert=1}\left\lVert\bm{\sigma}(\bm{m}_{j})\bm{\theta}\right\rVert>0
,
and hence the existence of some
\underline{w}_{k,\epsilon}>0
such that

c_{\epsilon}\geq\nu_{\alpha}[\underline{w}_{k,\epsilon},\infty)=(\underline{w}_{k,\epsilon})^{-\alpha}>0;

see (
2.8
).
On the other hand,
under the choice of
I=B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{k})
,
we have

\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}^{(1)|b}\big(\partial I)=0

due to the absolute continuity of measures
\nu_{\alpha}
and
\mathbf{S}
(see Assumption
2
).
This verifies the conditions in Theorem
B.1
,
allowing us to conclude that
c_{k,\epsilon}<\infty
and obtain (
E.43
).
∎

The next result provides an upper bound over the proportion of time that
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x})
is not close enough to a local minimum.

Lemma E.4
.

Given
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
,
it holds for all
t\in(0,1)
small enough that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\max_{i:\ \bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor s/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})\Big\}ds>t^{2}\bigg)<q^{*}t,

where
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is a constant that does not vary with
t
or
\epsilon
.

Proof.

There are only finitely many elements in
V^{*}_{b}
.
Therefore, it suffices to fix some
\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}
(recall that
I_{i}
is the attraction field associated with
\bm{m}_{i}
, and w.l.o.g. we assume
\bm{m}_{i}
= 0 in this proof) and prove that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor s/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\Big\}ds>t^{2}\bigg)<q^{*}t

(E.44)

holds for all
t>0
small enough, where
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is a constant that does not vary with
\epsilon
or
t
.

Let
T^{\eta}_{0}=0
, and (for all
k\geq 1
)

\displaystyle S^{\eta}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\{u>T^{\eta}_{k-1}:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\},\qquad T^{\eta}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\{u>S^{\eta}_{k}:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})\}.

Then, by defining
N^{\eta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\max\{k\geq 0:\ S^{\eta}_{k}\leq t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\}
,
we have

\displaystyle\#\Big\{u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\Big\}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N^{\eta}}T^{\eta}_{k}\wedge\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-S^{\eta}_{k}.

(E.45)

Next,
recall that
\alpha>1
is the heavy-tailed index in Assumption
2
,
and the time scaling
\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)
is defined in (
3.12
)
with
\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1}(\eta)
.
Fix some
\beta\in(0,\alpha-1)
, and let

\displaystyle k(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}1/\eta^{(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)+\beta}.

(E.46)

Given
\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})
,
define events (with
I_{i;\delta,M}
defined as in (
E.10
))

\displaystyle A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\in I_{i;\epsilon,M}\text{ for all }u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor\big\},

\displaystyle B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{\text{for each $j\leq k(\eta)$},\exists u\in[T^{\eta}_{j-1}+1,S^{\eta}_{j}]\text{ s.t. }\eta\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{u}\right\rVert>\delta\big\}.

On the event
B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})
,
note that

N^{\eta}\wedge k(\eta)\leq W^{\eta}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\#\{u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor:\ \eta\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{u}\right\rVert>\delta\}.

Next, define the event

\displaystyle F^{\eta}_{t}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\big\{k(\eta)>W^{\eta}\big\}.

On
B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\cap F^{\eta}_{t}
, we must have

\displaystyle N^{\eta}\leq W^{\eta}<k(\eta)=1/\eta^{(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)+\beta}.

Furthermore, given a constant
T\in(0,\infty)
,
let

E^{\eta}_{t,T}(\bm{x})\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\{T^{\eta}_{k}\wedge\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-S^{\eta}_{k}\leq T/\eta\ \forall k\geq 1\}.

On event
B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\cap F^{\eta}_{t}\cap E^{\eta}_{t,T}(\bm{x})
,
observe that

\displaystyle\#\{u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{N^{\eta}}T^{\eta}_{j}\wedge\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor-S^{\eta}_{j}\qquad\text{by \eqref{proof, property on N eta, 1, lemma: metastability, proportion of time not around local minima}}

\displaystyle\leq k(\eta)\cdot T/\eta=T/\eta^{1+\beta+(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)},

and hence

\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{I}\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor s/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\Big\}ds\leq\frac{T/\eta^{1+\beta+(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)}}{\lfloor 1/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}.

However, due to

\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1}(\eta)

and

\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1>(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)\cdot(\alpha-1)+1+\beta

(recall that we’ve fixed some
\beta\in(0,\alpha-1)
),
we have

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\frac{T/\eta^{1+\beta+(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)}}{\lfloor 1/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}=0.

In summary,
to prove (
E.44
),
it suffices to show that given
t,\epsilon>0
, there exist
\delta
and
T
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})\big)^{c}\Big)<q^{*}t,

(E.47)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\Big(\big(B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\big)^{c}\Big)=0,

(E.48)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(\big(F^{\eta}_{t}\big)^{c}\big)=0,

(E.49)

\displaystyle\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\Big(A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})\cap B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\cap F^{\eta}_{t}\cap\big(E^{\eta}_{t,T}(\bm{x})\big)^{c}\Big)=0,

(E.50)

where

q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is a constant that does not vary with
\epsilon
or
t
.

Proof of Claim (
E.47
)
.
This follows immediately from the first exit time analysis.
Specifically,
for each
\epsilon\in(0,\bar{\epsilon})
, we have

\big(A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})\big)^{c}\subseteq\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\notin I_{i;\bar{\epsilon},M}\text{ for some }u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor\}.

Then,
the properties (
E.2
) and (
E.19
) allow us to apply Theorem
B.1
under the choice of

I=I_{i;\bar{\epsilon},M}
and get

\displaystyle\limsup_{\eta\downarrow 0}\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x}))^{c}\big)\leq 1-\exp(-qt),\qquad\forall t>0,

where we set
q=\max_{j\in[K]}\widecheck{\mathbf{C}}_{j}\big((I_{j;\bar{\epsilon},M})^{c}\big)
(note that it does not vary with
\epsilon
or
t
).
Lastly, for any
t>0
small enough, we have
1-\exp(-qt)\leq 2qt
.
We conclude the proof by picking
q^{*}=2q
.

Proof of Claim (
E.48
)
.
By the strong Markov property at each
T^{\eta}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\big((B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x}))^{c}\big)\leq k(\eta)\cdot\underbrace{\sup_{\bm{y}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{y})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})\text{ for some }u<\tau^{>\delta}_{1}(\eta)\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p_{\delta}(\eta)},

where

\tau^{>\delta}_{1}(\eta)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}{}\min\{j\geq 1:\ \eta\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{j}\right\rVert>\delta\}.

Applying Lemma
B.4
,
it holds for any
\delta>0
small enough that

p_{\delta}(\eta)=\bm{o}\big(1/k(\eta)\big).

This concludes the proof of claim (
E.48
).

Proof of Claim (
E.49
)
.
Recall that
H(x)=\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{1}\right\rVert>x)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-\alpha}(x)
as
x\to\infty
,
and that

{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\in\mathcal{RV}_{\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}\cdot(\alpha-1)+1}(\eta)

as
\eta\downarrow 0
(see (
3.12
)).
Observe that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\big(\big(F^{\eta}_{t}\big)^{c}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\#\big\{u\leq\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor:\ \eta\left\lVert\bm{Z}_{u}\right\rVert>\delta\big\}\geq k(\eta)\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}\Big(\text{Binomial}\big(\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor,\ H(\delta/\eta)\big)\geq k(\eta)\Big).

For the expectation of the Binomial variable above, note that

\frac{t}{\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)}\cdot H(\delta/\eta)\in\mathcal{RV}_{-(\mathcal{J}^{*}_{b}-1)(\alpha-1)}(\eta)

as
\eta\downarrow 0
.
Then, Claim (
E.49
) follows from Markov’s inequality and the definition of
k(\eta)
in (
E.46
).

Proof of Claim (
E.50
)
.
On
A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})\cap B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})
,
we have
T^{\eta}_{k}\wedge\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor=\tilde{T}^{\eta}_{k}\wedge\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor
for each
k\geq 1
, where

\tilde{T}^{\eta}_{k}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\min\big\{u>S^{\eta}_{k}:\ \bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\notin I_{i;\epsilon,M}\setminus B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})\big\}.

Furthermore, it has been noted above that, on the event

B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\cap F^{\eta}_{t}
, we have
N^{\eta}\leq k(\eta)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\Big(A^{\eta}_{t}(\bm{x})\cap B^{\eta}_{\delta}(\bm{x})\cap F^{\eta}_{t}\cap\big(E^{\eta}_{t,T}(\bm{x})\big)^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\bm{x}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\big(\tilde{T}^{\eta}_{j}-S^{\eta}_{j}>T/\eta\text{ for some }j\leq k(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle\leq k(\eta)\cdot\underbrace{\sup_{\bm{y}\in B_{\epsilon}(\bm{0})}\mathbf{P}\big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{u}(\bm{x})\in I_{i;\epsilon,M}\setminus B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{0})\ \forall u\leq\lfloor T/\eta\rfloor\big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p^{*}_{T}(\eta)}.

The last step follows from the strong Markov property at the
S^{\eta}_{j}
’s.
Applying Lemma
B.2
,
we can find
T
large enough such that
p^{*}_{T}(\eta)=\bm{o}\big(1/k(\eta)\big)
as
\eta\downarrow 0
and complete the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Proposition
D.2
.

Proof of Proposition
D.2
.

The claim
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\Big(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor T/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{T}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert\geq\epsilon\Big)=0

has been verified by part
(ii)
of Lemma
E.3
.
In the remainder of this proof, we focus on establishing the claim

\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{d}_{L_{p}}^{{}_{[0,T]}}\Big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor{\bm{\cdot}}/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0}),\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x}_{0})\Big)\geq 2\epsilon\bigg)=0
.
W.l.o.g., in this proof we focus on the case where
T=1
,
and write
\bm{d}_{L_{p}}=\bm{d}_{L_{p}}^{{}_{[0,1]}}
to lighten notations.

We start with a few observations that allow us to bound

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(\eta)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\bigg(\bm{d}_{L_{p}}\Big(\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor{\bm{\cdot}}/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0}),\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}(\bm{x}_{0})\Big)\bigg)^{p}=\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}\underbrace{\int_{n/N}^{(n+1)/N}\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert^{p}dt}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\bm{d}^{(\eta)}_{p}(n)},

(E.51)

given a positive integer
N
.
First, for any
\eta>0
, let

\mathcal{I}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\mathbf{I}\big\{\bm{i}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)>1/N^{2}\big\},

where

\displaystyle\bm{i}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\int_{n/N}^{(n+1)/N}\mathbf{I}\bigg\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})\notin\bigcup_{j\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{j})\bigg\}dt,\qquad\forall n=0,1,\cdots,N-1.

That is,
\bm{i}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)
denotes the amount of time over
[\frac{n}{N},\frac{n+1}{N})
that the SGD iterates (under a
\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)
time scaling) are not close enough to any local minima,
and
\mathcal{I}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)
is the indicator that
\bm{i}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)>1/N^{2}
.
Moreover,
let

\displaystyle K^{(\eta)}_{N}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\mathcal{I}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n).

The proof hinges on the following claims:
there exist some
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
and a family of events
(A^{\eta}_{N})_{N\geq 1,\ \eta>0}
such that

1.

on the event
A^{\eta}_{N}
, we have
\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert\leq M
for all
t\leq\lfloor 1/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor
;

2.

it holds for all
N
large enough that
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}(A^{\eta}_{N})=1
;

3.

for all
N
large enough, there exists
\bar{\eta}=\bar{\eta}(N)>0
such that under any
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta})
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(K^{(\eta)}_{N}\geq j\ |\ A^{\eta}_{N})\leq\mathbf{P}\bigg(\text{Binomial}(N,\frac{2q^{*}}{N})\geq j\bigg),\qquad\forall j=1,2,\cdots,N.

To see why,
note that
by the definition in (
D.1
)–(
D.5
),
the process

\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})
only takes values in
\{\bm{m}_{j}:\ j\in[K]\}
,
so
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert<M\ \forall t>0
(see (
E.13
)).
Together with the Claim
(i)
above, we get
\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert\leq 2M
for all
t\in(0,1]
.
Moreover, note that
we must have
\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert<\epsilon

whenever

\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})\in\bigcup_{j\in[K]}B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{j}).

Then, the following holds on the event
A^{\eta}_{N}
for the terms
\bm{d}^{(\eta)}_{p}(n)
in (
E.51
):
if
\bm{i}^{(\eta)}_{N}(n)\leq 1/N^{2}
,
we have
\bm{d}^{(\eta)}_{p}(n)\leq\epsilon^{p}\cdot\frac{1}{N}+(2M)^{p}\cdot\frac{1}{N^{2}}
;
otherwise, we have the trivial bound
\bm{d}^{(\eta)}_{p}(n)\leq(2M)^{p}\cdot\frac{1}{N}
.
Therefore, on
A^{\eta}_{N}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(\eta)

\displaystyle\leq(2M)^{p}\cdot\frac{1}{N}+\sum_{n=1}^{N-1}\bm{d}^{(\eta)}_{p}(n)

\displaystyle\leq(2M)^{p}\cdot\frac{1}{N}+K^{(\eta)}_{N}\cdot\frac{(2M)^{p}}{N}+(N-1-K^{(\eta)}_{N})\cdot\Big(\frac{\epsilon^{p}}{N}+\frac{(2M)^{p}}{N^{2}}\Big)\leq(2M)^{p}\cdot\frac{2+K^{(\eta)}_{N}}{N}+\epsilon^{p}.

Then, given any
N
large enough,
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}(N))

and any

\beta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\bm{\Delta}(\eta)\geq\underbrace{\frac{2+2q^{*}+\sqrt{N^{\beta}}}{N}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}\delta(N,\beta)}\cdot(2M)^{p}+\epsilon^{p}\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}(K^{(\eta)}_{N}\geq 2q^{*}+\sqrt{N^{\beta}})=\mathbf{P}\big(\{K^{(\eta)}_{N}\geq 2q^{*}+\sqrt{N^{\beta}}\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\big)+\mathbf{P}\big(\{K^{(\eta)}_{N}\geq 2q^{*}+\sqrt{N^{\beta}}\}\setminus A^{\eta}_{N}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbf{P}\bigg(\text{Binomial}(N,\frac{2q^{*}}{N})\geq 2q^{*}+\sqrt{N^{\beta}}\bigg)+\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{N})^{c}\big)\qquad\text{by claim }(iii)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\text{var}\Big[\text{Binomial}(N,\frac{2q^{*}}{N})\Big]}{N^{\beta}}+\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{N})^{c}\big)\leq\frac{2q^{*}}{N^{\beta}}+\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{N})^{c}\big).

Lastly, to conclude the proof with
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}(\bm{\Delta}(\eta)>2^{p}\epsilon^{p})=0
,
note that

•

by claim
(ii)
,
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{N})^{c}\big)=0
;

•

due to
\beta\in(0,1)
we have
\lim_{N\to\infty}\delta(N,\beta)=0
,
and hence
\delta(N,\beta)\cdot(2M)^{p}+\epsilon^{p}<2^{p}\epsilon^{p}
eventually for all
N
large enough.

Now, it only remains to verify claims
(i)
,
(ii)
, and
(iii)
.

Proof of Claims
(i)
and
(ii)
.
We start by defining events
A^{\eta}_{N}
.
Let
t_{N}(n)=n/N
,

\displaystyle A^{\eta}_{N}(n)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\underbrace{\bigg\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t_{N}(j)/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})\in\bigcup_{\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}}B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{i})\ \forall j\in[n]\bigg\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}A^{\eta}_{N,1}(n)}\cap\underbrace{\Big\{\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert\leq M\ \forall t\leq t_{N}(n)\Big\}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}A^{\eta}_{N,2}(n)},

and let
A^{\eta}_{N}=A^{\eta}_{N}(N)
.
Note that
A^{\eta}_{N}(1)\supseteq A^{\eta}_{N}(2)\supseteq\cdots\supseteq A^{\eta}_{N}(N)=A^{\eta}_{N}
.
Claim
(i)
then holds by definition.
Furthermore,
by Lemma
C.3

and that
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}(\left\lVert\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{T}(\bm{x}_{0})-\hat{\bm{X}}^{\eta,\epsilon|b}_{T}(\bm{x}_{0})\right\rVert\geq\epsilon)=0
for any
T>0
,
we have

\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor t/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{x}_{0}):\ t>0\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f.d.d.}}{{\to}}\{\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}:\ t>0\};

then, since
\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{t}
only visits states in
V^{*}_{b}
,
we get
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(A^{\eta}_{N,1}\big)=1
for any
N\geq 1
.
On the other hand, part
(i)
of Lemma
E.3
implies
\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}\big(A^{\eta}_{N,2}\big)=1\ \forall N\geq 1
for any
M
large enough.
This verifies Claim
(ii)
.

Proof of Claim
(iii)
.
Let
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)\big)_{n\in[N-1]}

be a random vector
with law

\mathcal{L}\Big(\big({\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)\big)_{n\in[N-1]}\ \Big|\ A^{\eta}_{N}\Big).

It suffices to find some
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
such that,
for all
N
large enough, there is
\bar{\eta}=\bar{\eta}(N)>0

for the following claim to hold:
Given any
n\in[N-1]
and any sequence
i_{j}\in\{0,1\}\ \forall j\in[n-1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1\ \Big|\ \widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\Big)<2q^{*}/N\qquad\forall\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}).

(E.52)

To see why, under condition (
E.52
) and for any
\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta}(N))
,
there exists a coupling between
iid Bernoulli random variables
(\mathcal{Z}_{N}(n))_{n\in[N-1]}
with success rate
2q^{*}/N

and

(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n))_{n\in[N-1]}

such that

\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)\leq{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}(n)\ \forall n\in[N-1]

almost surely.
This stochastic comparison between
(\mathcal{Z}_{N}(n))_{n\in[N-1]}
and
(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n))_{n\in[N-1]}

directly verifies Claim
(iii)
.

To prove condition (
E.52
),
note that
given any
N
, any
n\in[N-1]
, and any sequence
i_{j}\in\{0,1\}\ \forall j\in[n-1]
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1\ \Big|\ \widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1;\ \widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\Big)}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\Big)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1;\ {\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\Big)\Big/\mathbf{P}(A^{\eta}_{N})}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\Big)\Big/\mathbf{P}(A^{\eta}_{N})}\qquad\text{by definition of }\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)\big)_{n\in[N-1]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1;\ {\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\Big)}\qquad\text{due to }A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\supseteq A^{\eta}_{N}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1;\ {\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}\cdot\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\Big)}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbf{P}\Big({\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(n)=1\ \Big|\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p^{\eta}_{1}(N)}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n)\Big)}{\mathbf{P}\Big(\big\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\big\}\cap A^{\eta}_{N}\Big)}}_{\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.06946pt\hskip-3.06946pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.06946pt\hskip-1.45833pt\raisebox{3.68855pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-1.45833pt\hskip 3.06946pt}}p^{\eta}_{2}(N)}.

For the term
p^{\eta}_{1}(N)
,
note that on the event
A^{\eta}_{N}(n)
we have
\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{t}(\bm{x}_{0})\in\bigcup_{\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}}B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{i})

at

t=\lfloor t_{N}(n)/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor
,
and hence (by Markov property)

\displaystyle p^{\eta}_{1}(N)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}}\sup_{\bm{y}\in B_{\epsilon/2}(\bm{m}_{i})}\mathbf{P}\bigg(\int_{0}^{1/N}\mathbf{I}\Big\{\bm{X}^{\eta|b}_{\lfloor s/\lambda^{*}_{b}(\eta)\rfloor}(\bm{y})\notin B_{\epsilon}(\bm{m}_{i})\Big\}ds>1/N^{2}\bigg).

Applying Lemma
E.4
,
for all
N
large enough there exist
\bar{\eta}=\bar{\eta}(N)>0
,
such that

p^{\eta}_{1}\leq q^{*}/N\ \forall\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta})
,
where
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
is a constant that does not vary with
N
or
\eta
.
As for the term
p^{\eta}_{2}(N)
,
note that for any event
B
with
\mathbf{P}(B)>0
,
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbf{P}(B\cap A^{\eta}_{N}(n))}{\mathbf{P}(B\cap A^{\eta}_{N})}\leq\frac{\mathbf{P}(B)}{\mathbf{P}(B)-\mathbf{P}\big((A^{\eta}_{N})^{c}\big)}\to 1,\qquad\text{ as $\eta\downarrow 1$, due to }\lim_{\eta\downarrow 0}\mathbf{P}(A^{\eta}_{N})=1.

(E.53)

Also, in the definition of
p^{\eta}_{2}(N)
above, note that there are only finitely many choices of
n\in[N-1]

and finitely many combinations for
i_{j}\in\{0,1\}\ \forall j\in[n-1]
.
By enumerating each of the finitely many choices for
B=\{{\mathcal{I}}^{\eta}_{N}(j)=i_{j}\ \forall j\in[n-1]\}

in (
E.53
),
we can find some
\bar{\eta}=\bar{\eta}(N)
such that

p^{\eta}_{2}(N)<2\ \forall\eta\in(0,\bar{\eta})

uniformly for all those choices.
Combining the bounds
p^{\eta}_{1}(N)<q^{*}/N
and
p^{\eta}_{2}(N)<2
,
we verify the condition (
E.52
) and conclude the proof.
∎

Appendix F
Properties of the Markov Jump Process
Y^{*|b}

Proposition F.1
.

Let Assumptions
1
and
5
hold.
The following claims hold for
((U_{j})_{j\geq 1},(V_{j})_{j\geq 1})
defined in (
E.7
):

1.

For any
t>0
,
\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j\leq i}U_{j}>t)=1
;

2.

For any
u>0
and
i\geq 1
,
\mathbf{P}(U_{1}+\cdots+U_{i}=u)=0
;

3.

\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Phi((U_{j})_{j\geq 1},(V_{j})_{j\geq 1})
holds for the mapping
\Phi
defined in (
C.4
); that is, it
is a continuous-time Markov chain
with initial distribution (
3.16
)
and
generator (
3.17
).

Proof.

(i)

Recall the definitions of
q_{b}(i)
and
q_{b}(i,j)
in (
3.14
).
Also, recall the definition of the discrete-time Markov chain
(S_{t})_{t\geq 0}
at the end of Section
3.1
,
with state space
\{\bm{m}_{1},\ldots,\bm{m}_{K}\}

and one-step transition kernel

\mathbf{P}(S_{t+1}=\bm{m}_{j}|S_{t}=\bm{m}_{i})=q_{b}(i,j)/q_{b}(i).

Note that the chain is well-defined due to (
E.5
).
We also introduce two notations.
First, we use
S_{t}(\bm{v})
to denote the Markov chain under initial condition
S_{0}(\bm{v})=\bm{v}
.
Second, for each
t\geq 0
, set
I^{S}_{t}(\bm{v})=i
if and only if
S_{n}(\bm{v})=\bm{m}_{i}
(i.e., recording the indices rather than the exact values of the states visited).

Let
\bm{x}_{0}
be the initial value prescribed in Theorem
3.2
,
and

i_{0}\in[K]
be the unique index with
\bm{x}_{0}\in I_{i_{0}}
.
Let
(E_{i})_{i\geq 0}
be a sequence of iid Exponential RVs with rate 1, which is independent of
(S_{t}(\bm{m}_{i_{0}}))_{t\geq 0}
.
By the law of
(U_{l},V_{l})_{l\geq 1}
specified in (
E.7
) (recall that
U_{1}=0
and
V_{1}=\bm{m}_{i_{0}}
),
for each
i\geq 2
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[i]}U_{j}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{j=0,1,\cdots,i-2}\frac{E_{j}}{q_{b}\big(I^{S}_{j}(\bm{m}_{i_{0}})\big)}\cdot\mathbf{I}\big\{S_{j}(\bm{m}_{i_{0}})\in V^{*}_{b}\big\}

(F.1)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{q^{*}}\cdot\sum_{j=0,1,\cdots,i-2}E_{j}\cdot\mathbf{I}\big\{S_{j}(\bm{m}_{i_{0}})\in V^{*}_{b}\big\}\qquad\text{ where }q^{*}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\max_{i:\ \bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}}q_{b}(i)\in(0,\infty)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{j=0}^{N_{i-2}}\frac{E_{j}}{q^{*}}\qquad\text{ where }N_{i}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{j=0}^{i}\mathbf{I}\big\{S_{j}(\bm{m}_{i_{0}})\in V^{*}_{b}\big\}.

Then, given
t>0
and positive integers
n,i
, we get

\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j\leq i}U_{j}>t)\geq\mathbf{P}(\sum_{j=0}^{n}E_{j}/q^{*}>t)\cdot\mathbf{P}(N_{i-2}>n).

To conclude the proof of part
(i)
,
it suffices to show that
for each
\epsilon>0
,
there exists
n=n(\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{n}E_{j}/q^{*}>t\bigg)>1-\epsilon,\qquad\lim_{i\to\infty}\mathbf{P}(N_{i}>n)=1.

(F.2)

The first claim holds for any
n
large enough due to
q^{*}\in(0,\infty)
; see (
E.5
).
The second claim follows from the irreducibility of the Markov chain
S_{t}(\bm{v})
; see Assumption
5
and (
E.6
).

(ii)

In light of the representation (
F.1
),
this claim is an immediate consequence of the absolute continuity of exponential distributions.

(iii)

We start by considering an equivalent representation of the continuous-time Markov chain
\bm{Y}^{*|b}
(recall the definitions in (
3.16
)–(
3.19
)),
based on the following straightforward observation:
the law of the process would remain the same if we allow the process to jump from any state
\bm{m}_{i}
to itself at exponential rates (i.e., by including Markovian “dummy” jumps where the process does not move at all).
More precisely,
using the mapping
\Phi
in Definition
C.4
,
we have

\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Phi((\tilde{U}_{k})_{k\geq 1},(\tilde{V}_{k})_{k\geq 1})

with
\tilde{U}_{k}
’s and
\tilde{V}_{k}
’s defined as follows.
Let
\tilde{V}_{1}
be sampled from the distribution
\theta_{b}(\cdot|\bm{m}_{i_{0}})
defined in (
3.15
)
and let
\tilde{U}_{1}\equiv 0
.
Next,
for any
t>0
,
l\geq 1
, and
m_{i},\ m_{j}\in V^{*}_{b}
(with possibly
m_{i}=m_{j}
),

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(\tilde{U}_{l+1}<t,\ \tilde{V}_{l+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ |\ \tilde{V}_{l}=\bm{m}_{i},(\tilde{V}_{j})_{j=1}^{l-1},\ (\tilde{U}_{j})_{j=1}^{l})=\mathbf{P}(\tilde{U}_{l+1}<t,\ \tilde{V}_{l+1}=\bm{m}_{j}\ |\ \tilde{V}_{l}=\bm{m}_{i})

\displaystyle=r^{*|b}(i,j)\cdot\big(1-\exp(-q_{b}(i)t)\big),

(F.3)

where

\displaystyle r^{*|b}(i,j)\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.7431pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Delta$}}\hskip-2.7431pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[K]:\ j^{\prime}\neq i}\frac{q_{b}(i,j^{\prime})}{q_{b}(i)}\cdot\theta_{b}(\bm{m}_{j}|\bm{m}_{j^{\prime}})

(F.4)

with
q_{b}(i)
and
q_{b}(i,j)
defined in (
3.14
).
That is,
by introducing “dummy” jumps from
\bm{m}_{i}\in V^{*}_{b}
to itself with exponential rate

\sum_{j^{\prime}\neq i}q_{b}(i,j^{\prime})\theta_{b}(\bm{m}_{i}|\bm{m}_{j^{\prime}})
,
we end up with the same process and obtain a reformulation
\bm{Y}^{*|b}_{\bm{\cdot}}\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 3.8889pt\hskip-3.8889pt\hbox{$=$}\hskip-3.8889pt\hskip-2.08144pt\raisebox{4.66875pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle d$}}\hskip-2.08144pt\hskip 3.8889pt}}\Phi((\tilde{U}_{k})_{k\geq 1},(\tilde{V}_{k})_{k\geq 1})
.

Meanwhile, we state a useful property of the mapping
\Phi
.
Recall that
U_{1}=0
,
and set
\hat{T}_{0}=1
. For each
k\geq 1
, define (under the convention
U_{0}=0
)
